#103100 299 2005-09-24T00:48:43Z blue_tetris
Quote: Keron Cyst, Sep 24 2005, 12:35 AM
"Religion?" Don't you mean "science?"
No. I meant religion. I think people should differentiate between God and religion. Religion is definitely real and was made by people. People are real and were made by God, if you believe in Him.

So, I posit: Ensure that your faith in God is deeper than your faith in religion. Trusting religion can lead you astray, but trusting God will not. I think people need to understand the difference between religiosity and spirituality--the latter being far more important and much less harmful to society.

EDIT: Oh, and by spiritual, I mean that you want to accept the idea that certain things may exist that defy nature (and are thus supernatural). A spiritual person believes in the existence of "spirits", as in souls, and other phenomena that are otherwordly.

Being a spiritual Christian means that you believe in God and the power of Christ, without accepting all the religious baggage those two guys tend to bring along with them. If you are spiritual, you can be a Christian with your spirit and accept evolution (and other science) with your logic. Also, Jews conflict with Christians in religious issues. Jew and Christians and other religious types, however, are also spiritual and believe that a supreme being is inherently good and wishes them to be good. This belief in an ultimately good force gives them something to agree upon. Their different religions only give them fear, hate, and prejudice.

That's the difference between religion and spirituality. Religion tends to include its own science, its own fears, its own hatred, its own laws, and its own prejudices. Spirituality is something that all religions can agree upon, and it doesn't shove war down our throats at inopportune times.

On the issue of Science, Belief and all that jazz......

I once had to run a chapel service for my school. (Ugh- what a great thing for someone who does not believe to do.) And so I tried to do something that-
a) Didn't go against any of my beliefs.
Wouldn't get me expelled.

Basically, I handled the mammoth task of dissecting "Science v/s Religion" and giving my own views... (Which were sorta skewed towards a perspective that would please the Lutherans of my school- once again, I didn't want to get expelled.)

I talked quite a bit about how to create a universe, highlighting how impossible it is, and everything. Just how improbable- nay, impossible- it seemed to be for that original singularity to
a) expand into the universe as we know it
Pop into existance in the first place

I gave quite a few figures of what happened at the birth of all things, and then moved onto the impossibility of The Beggining of Life. You know, alive things like you and I. Things that, sorta, by all rights shouldn't exist.
And so, then I moved on to how this relates to God. When you boil down to it, certain athiests whose opinions I truly do respect (Take Bill Bryson, who I once heard speak at a Literary Lunch (Yes, I did attend it by my own free will, but he is a great author, and hi-larious in his travel books as wel as speaking) about his views on Religion, when questioned on them)... ummmm.... I think I mucked up my sentance structure.
*cough*
Well, he said that, when you boiled down to it, Science can't give us the answers. It can't find reasons for why we're here, why ANY of this happened. And that, well, God is as good a reason as any.

Then I went on to talk a bit about my views on how Religion and Science are sorta incomparable (which I've blabbed about here quite a bit allready) and how one explains the WHY of life, the universe and everything, and the HOW of how it happened. They both explain two totally unrelated things, and handle totally different aspects. It's like comparing (forgive my oft- repeated phrase, I'm quite proud of it) comparing Statistics to Poetry. You just can't.

Then I introduced the Birt house choir, and brought up- accidently- a slide with the word "WHY" written on it in huge letters.

I finished by saying how none of this was neccesarily right, but they were my views. And then I got hit by the rotten fruit.

----------------------

In the beggining, there was nothing. Then God said "let there be light", and there was still nothing, but you could _see_ it.

#103452 305 2005-09-25T03:34:50Z Atilla
Quote: jflash, 2005-09-24T15:56:57Z
Yes that's true, and it was a big deal (menaing all of the rituals, etc.) back in th Old Testament. But, when Jesus died for us on the cross, that was the ultimate sacrifice, and thus we no longer have to make various sacrifices, such as animals. Those were done forever when Jesus died.

I'm not referring (specifically) to sacrifices and such. I'm saying that people sometimes go through the motions of religion without really believing. By rituals, I mean all the religious practices which are repeated regularly - saying grace, going to Eucharist, saying the "Our Father" and "Hail Mary" and so on. Because they are always the same, eventually the point may be reached when they are repeated because they are a habit, without thought of the true meaning. The holy words become empty because they are no longer believed, just spoken because it's something you do - like saying "Bless you" or "gesundheit" when someone sneezes. For that matter, the phrase "Oh my god" has become a general exclamation rather than a prayer or expression of belief (even I use "Good gods!" as an aid to communication sometimes, and I'm agnostic). And going to church is pointless if you just spend the whole time daydreaming, which is what many people do.

I hear often how Christians are arrogant and narrow-minded for thinking they have the only "ticket to heaven." Really, it is offensive. I understand.

I heard a funny quote recently,

"Debating on the internet is like competing in the special olympics...
even if you win, you're still retarded."

Not to say that debating like this is utterly fruitless, but I think it isn't too far fetched to say that it is more of a stage for putting on a show than anything else. The reason I say this is that... I wonder if there was ever a single person who was "saved" - converted to Christianity - because of an internet debate. Probably not! On the other hand, I would wager that there certainly have been some Christians who have wavered in their faith and backslidden or completely rejected christianity as a result of a debate like this. I think I finally understand why this is so.

Now, I really am not here to have a formal debate about this, because like I already noted, it is quite pointless. So please, take a minute to relax and simply read what I have to say. I would like to take a break from all the facts and data, and evolution and creation debating, because we have all heard it a hundred times and I believe it is getting quite old for everyone here. Besides, I know alot of you here are decent people, and I think you would agree with me that life is made up of alot more than facts and scientific data.

Salvation by grace through faith in Jesus Christ.... it is something that happens in the heart, if you know what I mean. One can't just learn fact A, B, and C, and then recite them, and say "okay, I must be saved now." Salvation is a supernatural regeneration of man's heart that was once completely dead to God. It is something that occurs in the head, the heart, the soul, and eventually the body. You could call it a new birth of sorts (hence the term - born again). I think then, the reason why it seems that Christians always lose online debates, is because we are sort of fighting on the wrong battlefield. It is good to defend what you believe, but it is useless if you are not first living what you believe.

I will be honest with everybody here... after all is said and done, and all the big scientific terms and theories and facts are thrown around, I am left with what I see in real life, right in front of me. I don't want to sound naive, but there is really no way not to: God is so real. (Please, please, hold off on the volley of facts against my experiential sandcastle.) You see, there is more to life than meets the eye. I think if many Christians would be honest, the one thing that "converted" them was not some amazing fact that they didn't know before. Rather, it was a personal experience - you could call it a revelation, of the saving grace of Jesus Christ and the reality of God's existence. Where does the revelation come from, you may ask? Why, the Holy Spirit - the person of God that is actually living within a Christian.

Besides a saving knowledge of God, which is revealed in one's heart upon salvation, there exist miracles which do occur and, to my knowledge, cannot be explained without reference to the supernatural. There are miracles in the Bible, yes, but there are also miracles today. I will just give you a couple examples of stuff I have seen for myself. You can take it or leave it. At a quasi-church informal gathering I used to attend in my old hometown (on friday nights) we would have regular times of prayer for people who were sick or had some kind of physical malady. Something didn't happen every single night, but there were a surprising amount of things that I did see. Some were little things.

One woman had had a fractured wrist for years that never healed properly, and she had been having pain whenever she used her wrist. The night we prayed for her... pain went away, feels "warmth" in wrist, everything is all better, etc. There was a person who was crippled with some kind of leg injury and had not walked for very long time. People gathered around the guy and prayed for him, and he literally stands up and walks, it was amazing. On a seperate occassion, a certain person who was partially deaf was prayed for and completely healed. These are just things I can remember off the top of my head.

Also, I have a very close friend, somebody I have known for a long time, who travelled to Africa as a missionary. I talked with her when she returned and she had some crazy stuff to tell me. They were evangelizing a small tribe of natives, many of whom were practicing black magic and the like. One day when they were in the middle of the tribe, they were just worshipping with music, and started praying for different people. My friend, Em, prayed and laid hands on somebody who had large open sores and wounds on his skin because of a very nasty disease. As he was prayed for, the wounds closed up before her eyes! The guy was completely healed of his disease. Other people who were with the team of missionaries were having similar experiences, and this was of course causing quite an uproar in the village, people's wounds closing up and all that. Needless to say, the fellow who was healed then had one of those personal revelations I was talking about earlier, and was saved. I won't go into the experience they had with the witch doctor (who, I might add, was acting very oddly the whole time), but it ended with a physcadelic demonic manifestation thingy, then he basically flopped to the ground and confessed Jesus Christ as Lord.

So... I really don't expect to convince anyone of anything, but I just wanted to share with you how I feel, kind of give you a glimpse of things from my perspective. I think I am also kind of bringing this whole thing away from evolution vs. creation and back on topic by answering the question posed in the title of the topic. It also seems kind of funny looking at the context in which I posted this. Maybe I look like I'm epitomizing the caricature of christianity that you were all laughing at earlier... but I dont know how long I'll be around here, and I just want to be open with you guys. Cheers.

#109787 336 2005-10-23T01:21:24Z kai
The origin of the Earth

The age of the Earth was once, and still is, a matter great debate. In 1650 Archbishop Ussher used the Bible to calculate that the Earth was created in 4004BC. Later on in the mid-nineteenth century Charles Darwin believed that the Earth must be extremely old because he recognised that natural selection and evolution required vast amounts of time.

It wasn't until the discovery of radioactivity that scientists began to put a timescale on the history of the Earth. Rocks often contain heavy radioactive elements which decay over long periods of time, the decay is unaffected by the physical and chemical conditions and different elements decay at different rates (These rates are slow and half-lifes of several hundred million years are not uncommon)

Throughout this century the race has been on to discover the oldest rocks in the world. The oldest volcanic rock found so far has been dated at 3.75 billion years old, but this is not the whole story. Meteorites created at the same time as the Earth hit us all the time, radioactive dating shows that they are about 4.55 billion years old.

THE EARLY ATMOSPHERE
The present composition of the atmosphere is: 21% OXYGEN
78% NITROGEN
0.04% CARBON DIOXIDE
~0.9% ARGON

The atmosphere wasn't like this when the Earth was created over 4½ billion years ago.

THE FIRST BILLION YEARS

The Earth's surface was originally molten, as it cooled the volcanoes belched out massive amounts of CARBON DIOXIDE, STEAM, AMMONIA and METHANE. There was NO OXYGEN. The STEAM condensed to form water which then produced shallow seas.

Evidence points to bacteria flourishing 3.8 billion years ago so this means that life got under way about 700 million years after the Earth was created. Such early forms of life existed in the shallow oceans close to thermal vents, these vents were a source of heat and minerals.

THE NEXT BILLION YEARS

These primitive life forms then took the next evolutionary step and started to PHOTOSYNTHESISE (using sunlight to convert carbon dioxide and water to food energy and oxygen). This was an important turning point in Earth history because the carbon dioxide in the atmosphere was being converted to oxygen.
These green plants went on producing oxygen (and removing the CO2).
Most of the carbon from the carbon dioxide in the air became locked up in sedimentary rocks as carbonates and fossil fuels. Carbon dioxide also dissolved into the oceans.
The ammonia and methane in the atmosphere reacted with the oxygen.
Nitrogen gas was released, partly from the reaction between ammonia and oxygen, but mainly from living organisms such as denitrifying bacteria. (remember that nitrogen is a very unreactive gas and it has built up slowly).

THE LAST 2½ BILLION YEARS OR SO

As soon as the oxygen was produced by photosynthesis it was taken out again by reacting with other elements (such as iron).This continued until about 2.1 billion years ago when the concentration of oxygen increased markedly. As oxygen levels built up and then . . . . . .
The ozone layer was formed which started to filter out harmful ultraviolet rays. This allowed the evolution of new living organisms in the shallow seas.

1 In the beginning God created the heavens and the earth. 2 Now the earth was formless and empty. Darkness was on the surface of the deep. God's Spirit was hovering over the surface of the waters.

3 God said, "Let there be light," and there was light. 4 God saw the light, and saw that it was good. God divided the light from the darkness. 5 God called the light "day," and the darkness he called "night." There was evening and there was morning, one day.

6 God said, "Let there be an expanse in the middle of the waters, and let it divide the waters from the waters." 7 God made the expanse, and divided the waters which were under the expanse from the waters which were above the expanse; and it was so. 8 God called the expanse "sky." There was evening and there was morning, a second day.

9 God said, "Let the waters under the sky be gathered together to one place, and let the dry land appear;" and it was so. 10 God called the dry land "earth," and the gathering together of the waters he called "seas." God saw that it was good. 11 God said, "Let the earth put forth grass, herbs yielding seed, and fruit trees bearing fruit after their kind, with its seed in it, on the earth;" and it was so. 12 The earth brought forth grass, herbs yielding seed after their kind, and trees bearing fruit, with its seed in it, after their kind; and God saw that it was good. 13 There was evening and there was morning, a third day.

14 God said, "Let there be lights in the expanse of sky to divide the day from the night; and let them be for signs, and for seasons, and for days and years; 15 and let them be for lights in the expanse of sky to give light on the earth;" and it was so. 16 God made the two great lights: the greater light to rule the day, and the lesser light to rule the night. He also made the stars. 17 God set them in the expanse of sky to give light to the earth, 18 and to rule over the day and over the night, and to divide the light from the darkness. God saw that it was good. 19 There was evening and there was morning, a fourth day.

20 God said, "Let the waters swarm with swarms of living creatures, and let birds fly above the earth in the open expanse of sky." 21 God created the large sea creatures, and every living creature that moves, with which the waters swarmed, after their kind, and every winged bird after its kind. God saw that it was good. 22 God blessed them, saying, "Be fruitful, and multiply, and fill the waters in the seas, and let birds multiply on the earth." 23 There was evening and there was morning, a fifth day.

24 God said, "Let the earth bring forth living creatures after their kind, livestock, creeping things, and animals of the earth after their kind;" and it was so. 25 God made the animals of the earth after their kind, and the livestock after their kind, and everything that creeps on the ground after its kind. God saw that it was good.

26 God said, "Let us make man in our image, after our likeness: and let them have dominion over the fish of the sea, and over the birds of the sky, and over the livestock, and over all the earth, and over every creeping thing that creeps on the earth." 27 God created man in his own image. In God's image he created him; male and female he created them. 28 God blessed them. God said to them, "Be fruitful, multiply, fill the earth, and subdue it. Have dominion over the fish of the sea, over the birds of the sky, and over every living thing that moves on the earth." 29 God said, "Behold, I have given you every herb yielding seed, which is on the surface of all the earth, and every tree, which bears fruit yielding seed. It will be your food. 30 To every animal of the earth, and to every bird of the sky, and to everything that creeps on the earth, in which there is life, I have given every green herb for food;" and it was so.

31 God saw everything that he had made, and, behold, it was very good. There was evening and there was morning, a sixth day.

#110083 355 2005-10-23T23:43:16Z iangb
Quote: nevermore, 2005-10-23T15:13:19Z
String theory... I've heard quite a bit about it, and I must admit I don't understand it at all. Are you an expert on the matter? I'd really appreciate a real explanation of it.
I'm no expert, but I'm starting my physics degree at uni this year, and on of the books we had to read was called The Elegant Universe, by Brian Greene, which was basically all about string theory. I definitely reccomend it.

String theory was created to solve the fundamental problem in modern physics - that General relativity and Quantum Mechanics do not both work together. Normally this is not a problem, because General Relativity (GR) is for big/heavy things, whereas Quantum Mechanics (QM) is for small, lightweight things. But for certain cases, such as for black holes, neither theory works properly. This is because GR assumes that the fabric of spacetime is more or less uniform. Gravity bends spacetime, and so on. QM however says that at a small scale the fabric of spacetime is a 'quantum froth' - a constantly changing, wildly unpredictable... froth.
String theory solves the problem by saying that instead of fundamental substances and forces being made of point-particles, they are instead made of strings that vibrate in different ways, giving each string properties such as mass and charge. The clever bit is that a string is too large to be affected by the 'quantum froth', so the extra problems can be safely ignored.

There is a lot more complications (such as the theory predicts 11 dimensions, with the extra ones 'curled up' so they cannot be detected), but that is the basic theory.

Then there are lots of interesting inferences from the theory that explain many unanswered questions - why gravity is a much weaker force than the other three, and a bit about the big bang and why we live in a three (plus time) dimensional universe. So yeah, it's all fun.
